Uh cash and accrual is basically two different methods of running your reports You know, let's keep it simple two different methods of running your reports

[00:19:45] Um, and you can look at your reports in in two ways cash Okay, which basically means the income and expenses are recorded when the money changes hands Okay, so if if I go if i'm a, you know painter I paint houses and I go paint someone's house And they give me money the same day That's that I record that income today Okay, but If I paint their house and I give them an invoice and I say pay me in 30 days well now on the accrual basis Accrual basis is when I gave them the invoice the date of the invoice. So it's the date that I painted the um Did the painting But if I look at the the income on a cash basis, I don't record that income until I get paid So it might be 30 days later So accrual is all about timing Okay, cash Records income and expense when the money changes hands Accrual Records income and expense when the work has been performed Okay, whether or not we've been paid yet Or when with expenses whether or not we've paid those expenses yet. So invoices and bills invoices and bills are the two most common types of Accrual basis transactions Because think about it with invoices If i'm doing the bookkeeping for a client Now we don't use invoices. So this is a bad example If you're a painting company and I do work for a client and I give them an invoice They're going to pay me later Okay, so under cash basis, I have not recognized any income just yet

[00:21:21] Okay, and if i'm paying bills if somebody gives me a bill For work that they performed Under cash basis. I haven't paid that bill yet. I might pay it in 30 days Okay, so it's just a different way of looking at things. And so the reason I say both is because um With like we don't make the decision. We don't make the decision necessarily Accrual basis Financials always give us a better idea of how the client's business is performing Okay, because there's uh What what the goal in um, like accrual based bookkeeping? Um is is that we want to want to match income With the expenses that were incurred to generate that income. Okay, think about painting a house Okay, you paint the house Well, you gotta If i'm if i'm a painter and I paint somebody's house I've got to pay a bunch of people to do the work. I've got to buy the supplies I'm i've got all these expenses Okay, and so let's say I pay for all those expenses And then we go paint the house And then I send an invoice to the client and say hey pay me in 30 days Okay, well under cash basis Let's say this month i've got all these expenses, but they don't pay me until next month So I don't see the income on my reports until next month And so if I look at my reports this month is going to look like I lost money Next month is going to look like I made a bunch of money because it doesn't have any expenses associated with it but if we use accrual basis reports now We recognize those expenses this month and we also recognize the income this month because that's the date that the work was performed

[00:22:57] And that's the date that the invoice was created Okay, so now we're matching things up We're matching the income that was generated with the expense that was incurred to generate that income And that gives us a better idea of how our business is performing than if they were to happen in two different months You know and that skews the numbers Okay, so what ends up happening is that quickbooks does all the work So if your client you you know is using invoices and bills and things like that quickbooks already tracks this stuff You just select that toggle that radio button at the top of your reports to view the reports in cash basis or in accrual basis Okay, and this is a great opportunity for me to say hey if that was like what in the world is bill talking about?
